PRIME — Pay-per-view

Colossus IV, 5 August 2007

5 August 2007
Content noticeA period document, served as recovered; withheld details are marked. It carries the humor, language, and content typical of its era and community — some of it crude, dated, or offensive, including material that wouldn't be written today. See Curation.
Colossus IV Safeco Field: Seattle, Washington ---- INTRO First it was Tony Rolo overcoming Ignatius Lisieux to win his second ever Global Championship… becoming the first man to hold PRIME’s top honor on two occasions. The image of Tony Rolo rolling up Lisieux with a simple small package plays in slow motion One year later, it was Hoyt Williams reaching the top of the mountain by defeating the reigning Universal Champion, Killean Sirrajin. Hoyt Williams is shown hitting the Crucified and Saved, then hooking the leg as the ref counts three and a heavenly glow illuminates the scene. Last year… in a match four years in the making… Tchu won his second Universal Championship by defeating the once again reigning champion, Killean Sirrajin. Tchu drives Killean’s skull into the mat with the Dowfall, then makes the cover on a dark night, in the pouring rain of Tampa, Florida. This year… Lindsay Troy looks to break the Colossus curse and become the first Universal Champion to successfully defend the most coveted prize in the business today. Standing between her and destiny? Her former partner… her old acquaintance… her nemesis… Sonny Silver. And he has a taste for gold. Three years in the making… Lindsay Troy Vs. Sonny Silver… The image of the two superstars fades away to a screen of black ~ ~ ~ ~ ~ Fireworks erupt at an angle, traveling up the sides of two giant vertical screens that rest at a 70 degree angle, read ‘PRIME’ in striking blue. Between then, the massive Wal*Tron, custom design for Colossus… and below… the entrance way arrives between a 12 foot tall Roman Numeral ‘I’ and ‘V’, appearing to be carved from stone. Above the ‘IV’, a giant Stone ‘C’ rests, its curve etching the edge of the Wal*Tron. All told, the structure stands maybe some sixty feet tall, an epic sight, reaching high towards the roof of Safeco Field where the fireworks erupt into colorful bursts. Thousands and thousands of PRIMEates scream as loud as they can… eagerly anticipating the start of CIV. As the roar echoes through all of Seattle, the camera zooms around the audience, catching the smiling faces of the massive crowd. The camera then spins towards the ring. White ropes travel between stunning silver ring posts. And in the center of the squared circle stand PRIME’s official announcer. Vince Howard: Ladies and Gentlemen… Welcome to Colossus IV!!!! Tonight, your support has helped to set a brand new attendance record for Safeco Field of 56,004 (yeah, we crammed them in there)! Nick: What an unbelievable number! And welcome everyone to Seattle, Washington and Safeco Field…hosts to PRIME’s fourth annual Colossus! Richard: This is it… the biggest show of the year, baby! And what a night its gonna be! Nick: We’ve got nine incredible matches lined up for you tonight, including the triple main event! Every title will be on the line… feuds will come to an end, grudges will be settled… and legends will be born! Richard: Not to mention a special surprise Blaine Blair claims awaits us all. Nick: No doubt, what a night its going to be! ---- Mike Wade and Jack Murphy vs. Chandler Tsonda and the Illustrious Face Eater The Safeco Field erupts into a cacophony of boos and hisses all directed towards the entryway the very instant the shittiest song ever to grace the music scene. "Da Funk," even shittier than before with rehashed REMIX action, denotes the entrance of two-thirds of the Classiest Group of Level-Headed Individuals on the PRIME ROSTER: Chandler Tsonda and the Illustrious Face-Eater. Facey holds his hands up in a "wtf?" manner in regards to their reaction, but after a quick word from Chan he just as soon dismisses them. Instead, he directs his enthusiasm to his partner, encouraging Chan to high-five him repeatedly as they walk to the ring. The consummate team player, Chandler, offers back a lackluster high-five, to which Face shakes his head. Nick: Might be important to point out that this is Tsonda's first PPV action since the fall of 2006. This isn't on the Model Citizen's mind, as he discusses "strategy" w/ the Face. Despite his new "corporate" persona, his attire isn't all that different. Same fruity mesh t-shirt, same white track paints, same Gucci trainers. The Face-Eater, however, has moved away from his traditional singlet-and-cape combo. The mask is still there; the top cropped and allowing his long and dirty black hair to fall loose. Even stranger is the fact that he's wearing a fully tailored navy blue Burberry suit. Denzel Washington is missing a piece of his wardrobe, somewhere. Richard: I like it. These guys have class AND style. Nick: Not exactly practical wrestling attire, but if it's what he prefers... The duo step into the ring, ever so confident with both of their Colossus records standing at 1-and-0. Last year, both men held victories over their opponents (Tsonda over Noble, Facey over Nova) and this year they look to continue that tradition. The A-Listers play the "let's ignore our opponent" game as the arena goes slightly darker and blue lights hit the entrance area. Whoever appeals to the law against his fellow man is either a fool or a coward. Whoever cannot take care of themselves without that law is both. For a wounded man shall say to his assailant, 'If I live, I will kill you. If I die, You are forgiven.' Such is the rule of honor. And out walks the Bull, to quite the impressive pop. If nothing else, PRIME appreciates a "kick you in the throat and then do your mom" brawler like Murphy. The guitar riffs provide the perfect ambiance as he glares down at the ring, hoping to give Adam Dick the new name "The Illustrious Paraplegic". Richard: If I had to pick a guy I like to shatter a fibula tonight, it'd be Jack Murphy. Nick: You've gotta wonder how this will play out, with his partner being Mike Wade. Richard: Well they DID wrestle together in AWC. And you saw what happened last week! Wade and Face are done! Wade just let that potato-smelling vagrant tear through his partner. Nick: Well, speak of the devil, here comes Jack's partner for the evening. Mike Wade begins hobbling down the ramp, causing Jack to stop halfway and wait for him. Mike looks excited, delivering a stern look to the ring and his opponents until he reaches the Bull. Mike Wade: What happened?! I thought we were going to enter together! Like a team! Jack Murphy: But we aren't a team--we're one-time partners. Mike Wade: Come on, Jack! Where's the solidarity! The Bull merely ignores the comments and proceeds to walk to the ring, Wade enthusiastically following. The two climb in, with both Mike and Jack giving the Face-Eater particularly disheartening looks. In the ring, the A-Listers are snickering at their opponents. They also decide to go rock, paper, scissors to decide who starts the match. Facey throws rock. Tsonda throws... Richard: MY GOD! Did he just throw "gun?!" Nick: Oh c'mon, that's the most immature thing of all time! Facey: GOD DAMN IT! I KNEW I SHOULD HAVE GONE WITH EARTHQUAKE. Having lost the RPS, Facey grumbles as Tsonda heads the corner. They're still both ignoring the fact that Wade and Murphy are now inside the ring. Wade tries to catch Facey's glance with his glare, but no dice. Nick: Who wants to get at Facey more? Murphy or Wade? The answer: Jack Murphy. He gives Wade a slight nudge back towards the corner, which brings a smile to the Irishman's face. He knows the punishment that the Bull has in store for the Face. Richard: Does Murphy have human emotions? Or is he just a robotic ass-kicker? Tsonda isn't even out on the curtain, nor is Facey even paying attention to what's going on, when Jack Murphy quickly charges across the ring, complete with Instant Pummel Action! Nick: Murphy wasting no time and taking charge! By three blows, Adam Dick has been struck back into his corner, taken completely off guard. Chandler, forced to pay attention, looks at Jack very offended and asks. Chandler Tsonda: Dude, what the fuck! Jack drives an elbow to his face for good measure before whipping him to the opposite corner with brute strength. Facey flies to the corner with the characteristics of a rag-doll, bouncing like a Spartan who just took a Plasma to his Warthog (eat that ref, [name withheld]). Wade tries to sneak a hit in, but the Face's collision forces him back too quickly. He's quickly taken to the mat for the first time in this contest as Jack levels the Face-Eater with a Bulldog. Richard: Come on! That move is so 1998! Not a moment spared, Murphy is quick to his feet and pacing around the ring. He's too focused on sending a message to his foe to notice the crowd's encouraging cheers. He continues to assault his foe, driving boots to his body as Face tries to crawl to his corner. Jack grips him by his left ankle, pulling him back to the center. The maneuver allows Adam enough time to drive his other leg into Jack's face and knock him back. The blow, taking Murphy off-guard, only serves to piss him off even more. He pounces upon Facey and quickly lifts him to his feet. Jack only serves to drive him back down, forcibly flipping him to his back with a Hip-Toss. For a moment, Jack turns to look at his corner, noticing Mike Wade making a commotion in an effort to catch his attention. Nick: Mike Wade is dying for that tag! The Face-Eater sits up immediately, selling the pain like a champion and giving these asshole fans exactly what they want. He notices the impending Murphy's march and turns to see him near. Not wanting to get his ass kicked anymore, he rolls out of the bottom ropes and to the outside of the ring where Chandler joins him in an effort to regroup. Nick: Guess the Face-Eater needs to take a breather. Richard: You have MASTERED the powers of Observation, Nick. Bravo. The referee's count only reaches THREE! by the time Murphy gets fed up with their antics and the duo from the inside of the ring. He calls for Tsonda to "get his bitch-partner back into the ring." Facey obliges, but not before Chandler manages to get a few words in along the lines of "I ain't nobodies' bitch. Facey and Jack meet in the center, locking-up. Jack's power advantage is easily displayed here as he forces Adam into a headlock. Richard: His fragile neck! He's wrenching it with his drunken Irish strength! Once again the Eater is forced to the ropes, only this time he ducks the Bull's obvious attempt at a clothesline. Of course, Murphy anticipates this and is merely luring him in. Face tries to boot him in the stomach but Jack catches his foot. For a split second the Face-Eater catches his nemesis winking at him, but just as quickly he is taken down with a stiff clothesline. Nick: That almost took his head off! Mike Wade: MURPH! Jack turns to see his partner, standing on the middle rope with his body hanging halfway in the ring and his hand fully extended to take the tag. Mike Wade: TAG ME IN! Facey looks up from the mat, his eyes adjusted to the bright lights from the rafters, to notice Jack with his back towards him. Jack can only look at his partner with contempt, still not satisfied with the damage he's inflicted. The look he gives his partner says the words, "Not yet." He turns back to Facey... Nick: Dropkick from the Face-Eater that drives Jack Murphy back to his corner. Richard: FINALLY, some offense. A slap is heard, and Mike Wade leaps into the ring. Nick: Did Mike Wade just tag himself in? Richard: Jack got close enough, and Wade slapped his back! Jack doesn't fully comprehend what has happened as Wade enters the ring, so he gets in his face. Wade tries to explain that a tag was made, that he slapped Jack on the back and now he's in. Jack looks to the ref, who merely backs Wade in saying a clean tag was made. Nick: Some miscommunication here. Jack stares a hole through Wade, staying perfectly still for a moment. He then shakes his head in disgust and shoulders past Wade, returning to the corner. Mike points back to his partner walking away from him, gesturing to the crowd "Can you believe this feckin' guy?" Not a split second later does he deliver the back of Jack Murphy's head with a sickening spinning heel kick. Nick: MIKE WADE JUST ATTACKED THE BULL! Richard: What do you expect? No man should put up with the kind of disrespect Jack shows. As Jack falls into the corner Wade continues to putting the boots to him. All the while, the Face-Eater watches from the other side, smiling. Chandler Tsonda, standing behind him? Smiling too. Chan enters the ring and both men go to approach Wade delivering the beating. The confused referee turns away from pulling Wade off of his partner and instead tries to stop the approach of the A-List. Facey assures him everything is kosher and the two go to Wade's side, admiring the work he's doing. Nick: Oh... I can't believe it. They had us, AGAIN! Richard: What do you mean?! Nick: Are you dense, Richard?! Don't you get what's going on? Mike turns behind him, noting Chandler and Facey's presence behind him. He smiles and finally halts in his beating of Jack to give the two men hugs. Richard: Oh, I GET IT! Nick: Dammit! We fell for it again, Rich! Richard: THE A-LIST JUST GOT ONE MAN STRONGER. The three turn from Jack and call for Vince Howard to toss them a microphone. He obliges, straight-lining it to the palm of Mike Wade, which he immediately raises to his mouth. Mike Wade: MY NAME IS 'HIS SWERVINESS' FOR CHRIST'S SAKE! The fans reaction is that of hisses and boos. Not enough people have finished with their concessions to begin their rain of trash, but that doesn't mean a few full beer cups don't fly at the collected group. Mike Wade: Y'know, we planned on doing this to those two clowns that like to go even more ridiculous over the top than we, but come on, Jack. You fell right into our hand AGAIN. It was an opportunity we had to capitalize on. Facey takes the mic. The Illustrious Face-Eater: Damn right, Mike. Aren't you guys getting sick of this shit, yet?! When will you realize that we will fuck with your brains on a consistent basis?! AIN'T NO WAY AROUND IT, FOLKS. Play with fire, get burnt. Jack Murphy will continue to be taught this lesson but his inbred smelly heritage will prevent him from learning the concept. Before their verbal tirade can continue the trio are cut off. No, not by Jack Murphy getting up and eating all of their heads. He sits in the corner patiently. He expected something like this, which is why he bides his time on the mat as music begins to play. "LOUD AND ANGRY," by a band whose name you have to copy/paste, the [name withheld]. Ian Nackedy and Gildenstern enter the arena and something is obviously odd. The two's gimmick this time? Nick: Hopefully, Jack Murphy can get some aid! Richard: United by common enemies, they are~! Nick: Rich, it's YOUR astute observations that continue to amaze me. Former PRIME Superstars and Hall of Famers, Crudely Civilised. Joey Troy and Tony Rolo. Ian takes charge as Joey Troy, donning the same tights and tape attire of the former Global Champion. Gil stands to the back of him, looking the part of 'The Specialist' Tony Rolo. Ian has his own mic of course and begins addressing the Listers in the ring. Ian Nackedy: We are SICK and TIRED of being left off the card while you ungrateful tit-heads stink up THE GREATEST SHOW OF THE YEAR with your stupid antics! These people don't care about another A-List turd dropping in the pond! THEY WANT TITTIES! AND VAGS! DO YOU GUYS WANT TO SEE TITTIES AND VAGS?! Richard: He is Joey Troy! Ian Nackedy: You wouldn't know what we speak of, though, you HomoGays. All you know is circle-jerks and Boys Gone Wild DVDs! You talk about going to school?! Well, WE are going to take you to school. Chandler Tsonda: Apparently, you two cockrodeo clowns misread the directions to your next dark match. We’re the A-List, who the fuck are you? The Illustrious Face-Eater: You know what? You wanna get your face turned inside out like the Potato Man over here? GET IN THE RING, then. These things are never fun when the numbers aren't even, but don't think that has anything to do with the Unfair Advantage you fuckers are walking in with. Gil pokes Ian in the stomach and whispers. Gildenstern: Dude, that's the guy that threatened to stab me! Ian Nackedy: Don't sweat it, Gil! We're going to stomp those fools, and after we win... TITTIES AND VAGS FOR EVERYONE! LET'S SHOW EVERYONE THE MEANING OF THE TERM 'PAY PER VIEW ONDEMAND!' The referee, obviously bewildered with the events, simply shrugs and calls for the bell to be rung as the Crude Sound & the Civilised Fury take their places at ringside. Chandler instructs his partners to the outside as Ian encourages Jack to get up. Still trying his best to finish things himself, Jack Murphy rises slowly. Chandler Tsonda is right in his face with an elbow, but the Bull catches the elbow, using his superior upper body strength to force Tsonda back towards the ropes. Chandler senses Murphy trying to gain some momentum so he gives into Murphy's pushing then uses the ropes to reverse himself. However, Murphy reverses what effectively becomes Tsonda's Irish Whip and sends the Model Citizen to the opposite ropes. Unbeknownst to Murphy, Tsonda is a fast little critter. Quicker than the Bull expected, Chandler is back from the ropes and responds with a falling dropkick to the chest. Richard: What a specimen! I haven't seen kicks like this since Chuck Norris. Nick: The chief export of Chuck Norris is Pain. Richard: You're just so hip to it, Nick. Despite some of their better inclinations, the fans start to chant for Murphy, realizing that they need someone to get behind because hey, it's fawking Colossus. Realizing a mismatch when he sees it, Tsonda gives Mike Wade his official in-ring A-List introduction with a tag. The boos are tremendous, considering that this is the opening match. The fans don't give a flying hoot, though, because they're totally not okay with the A-List growing in membership. Nick: Mike Wade, how we forgot ye. Richard: And the awesomeness ye contain. Coming in like a bat out of hell, Wade charges Murphy and floors him with a running DDT. He climbs onto Murphy and hooks the leg for a pin. One... Two... ThrNO! Nick: Murphy simply can't keep this up. He's GOT to use his partners! Richard: Sadly, they're both massive tools. Still, in the middle of the ring and facing an opponent with fresh(er) legs, Murphy getting out of the ring is easier said than done. He reaches his feet, only to meet several right hands from Mike Wade. The Irishman follows it up with a kick to the gut and tries to grab Murphy for his finisher, but Murphy fights off with his hands. He tries to shoulder block his foe, but Wade is one step faster. He sidesteps back and deftly pulls up Murphy's arms, then in one motion sets up and executes a double underhook DDT. Richard: Wade doesn't get enough credit for how he does his thug thizzle in the ring. Nick: His choice of tag team partner and now choice of group membership overshadow his in-ring strengths. Wade drops a fist into Murphy's midsection, bringing a resounding "oof!" from the Bull. He follows the fist up with another cover, knowing that Murphy can't last much longer. One... Two... ThreeNO! Richard: Prolonging the inevitable. The crowd is now rambunctiously hoping for Murphy to make it to his corner, racketing up their cheers and bringing out small isolated chants of "MUR-PHY" . He's ignorant of these cheers, but they do seem to have a small effect on his morale, allowing him to keep on truckin'. Wade needs to stifle this, so he lifts Murphy up by the head and starts raining down fists to the Bull's back. Each time Murphy crumples, though, Wade pulls him back up and lets go more shots to the back. Nick: Very noble effort here from the A-List. Richard: I forgot; it's not a wrestling match, but a nobility contest. And then a funny thing happens. Wade lets out a vicious right elbow to the back, but Murphy doesn't budge. Wade lets another one go and still no movement. And all of a sudden, the Bull explodes upwards, flooring Wade with a spear. He's exhausted and despite his fierce competitive streak, he realizes that he may need a blow. He looks upon both of his allies, dressed like PRIME legends, and reluctantly slaps the open palm of Gildenstern, who hops into the ring eagerly, grinning like his PRIME legend of choice. Richard: Rolo and Troy all over. Oh joy. Wade's eyes go a little bit bigger as he sees the clear-cut biggest man in the match hop into the ring. He smartly slinks over to his corner and tags in the Face-Eater, who's already jawing at Gildenstern. The tag is applied and Facey comes in. He tries a flying body press at the big man, but unsurprisingly, Gildenstern catches him and deposits him violently on the mat with a scoop slam. G'Stern tries to follow this up with a slow, calculated knee drop. Facey escapes the move, though, by contorting his body, twisting up and kicking at the midsection of his opponent. Using his seemingly limitless energy, the Face hops up and drops a scissors kick on the back of Gildenstern's skull. Nick: Gildenstern's got size, but the Face has...umm...well...the panache, I guess. Richard: And alllll the babes. While the big man recovers, Facey reaches the turnbuckle. His back turned to Gildenstern, he haphazardly throws himself off the turnbuckle with a makeshift moonsault body press. The move itself is quite impressive and despite the lack of hangtime, he catches Gildenstern full on, basically falling into a pin. One... Two... ThrNO! Nick: Was that...a...wrestling move? Richard: Last year, he explodes; this year, he uses wrestling moves. My god, he's a trendsetter. Facey lets out a huge raspberry at the fact of that last attempt being thwarted. He hops back up and starts unleashing kicks on the prone Gildenstern. It looks like the big man is in dire straits, but he finally catches Facey's leg and pulls Dick down with a very crude dragon screw. He then grabs the Face's legs and slingshots him into the corner, where luckily Facey's two partners are waiting and catch him before he goes headfirst into the turnbuckle. Richard: Look at the camaraderie! They all look at each other and then, Three Ninjas-style, spring into badass action. Facey hops up onto the turnbuckle, then the other two A-Listers from the outside make hand-saddles, which he steps into. As Gildenstern turns around, Wade and Tsonda give the ol' heave ho and LAUNCH Face about twelve feet into the air, where he begins flapping his arms to try and stop his momentum. Nick: What...in the world? He sees the he's going to overshoot his opponent so he leans back, which puts him on a collision course with Gildenstern's neck. Thanks to his lean, he's already primed for the move that's coming; as his legs catch around G'Stern's neck, Face unravels his body downward, pulling his opponent with him and finishing up a vicious hurricanrana. Richard: I'm speechless. Nick: Folks, here live at Colossus IV, I can honestly say I've never seen a successful two-person launch hurricanrana. Having taken a decent beating from the move, the Face crawls slowly and attempts a pin. One... Two... Three... Richard: GAH! Nick: Gildenstern just barely got the shoulder up. He and Ian Nackedy must have known they were in for a rough night when they chose to go against these wily veterans. Having escaped the pin, Gildenstern starts crawling towards his corner. The crowd begins a steady clap and his partner slaps the turnbuckle in turn. Nick: For these two newcomers, it's gotta be a huge moment. This Seattle crowd is fifty thousand strong behind them. Richard: Yay, their first chokejob! With the momentum of the fans behind him, Gil inches forward and slaps the hand of Ian Nackedy, who explodes in the ring with a fury. FUCK THAT 'NO PUN INTENDED' BULLSHIT; PUN EXPLICITLY INTENDED. Face-Eater, though still reeling from the sacrificial huricanrana, is ready for the charging Nackedy. The two exchange blows and Nackedy takes the upper hand, then unleashes that ridiculous thing where the Rock looks at his hand and smacks the other guy. Except the other guy is waiting there just shaking his head and then knees Nackedy in his lower Nackedys, if ya catch my drift. Nick: What is that?! Richard: Cheating. Nick: Well…uhh…why are they using it? Richard: To win. Obviously. Nick: It ain’t right! With Ian bent over, the Face takes his opportunity to tag out, trying to keep the A-List one step ahead of the Bull & Fury. He slaps Tsonda’s hand and the Sultan of Style comes in with a bang, climbing the ropes and then entering the ring with a springboard helicopter kick. Suffice to say, it takes both men down. Richard: This has been a hell of an opener. Nick: Colossus is not your run-of-the-mill PPV. Tsonda’s a little bit quicker to his feet, seeing as the brunt of his fall took place on the body of Ian Nackedy. Nackedy is rising much more slowly and as he does, Tsonda grabs him in a side headlock, parading around the ring like an idiot. The crowd boos in turn and Tsonda begins twisting the headlock, putting pressure on his foe’s neck. Then he runs at the ropes and propels himself up, twists, and executes a nasty Tornado DDT, immediately following it with a cover. One… Two… Three… The ref’s hand stops inches from the mat as Jack Murphy makes the save with a stomp to Tsonda’s back. The ref also turns and tries to corral Murphy back into his corner. At this time, Face jumps in the ring as well. Tsonda whips Nackedy at the ropes and then monkey tosses to the mat. This is immediately followed up with a dual-action move: as Tsonda hits a legdrop across Nackedy’s throat, Facey drops a well-placed fist to the nuts. Nick: Nackedy’s nether regions are not doing well in this match. Richard: Probably feels like his interactions with chicks then. ZING! The ref turns back around just as Face is leaving the ring. Tsonda lifts Nackedy up and tosses him at the A-List corner. Nackedy looks pretty dazed, just lying in the corner. Tsonda makes the crude peace sign with a tongue inside sign to the other corner (international sign for eating box, dog). Murphy takes the bait and tries to jump in the ring again, bringing the ref back over to keep him in his corner. Richard: Haha, simply amazing. Tsonda looks over his shoulder and tags Mike Wade in, then slides out of the ring. Wade steps through the rings as the ref continues to try and explain some very complex rules to the Bull. Wade lifts Nackedy up in a vertical suplex and then brings him swinging down, crashing to the mat. Suddenly, Tsonda has a stroke of brilliance. He sees the ref turning around and realizes that he didn’t see the tag. Thus, the Model Citizen climbs up the turnbuckle and nods to Mike Wade, who catches on and winks bank. Nick: Great plan, too bad Tsonda’s move will have no effect. He’s not legal.. Richard: Hee hee hee… Nick: What? Richard: Hmmph….oh nothing. With the grace of performing a move practiced hundreds of times, Tsonda’s comes flying off the turnbuckle with a moonsault that hits a solid 8/10 on the flashbulbs going off scale. The impact of him on the fallen Nackedy shakes the ring. Richard: MODEL CITIZEN, BOYEE! Nick: I don’t like where I see this going… Despite that he’s not what we might call "legal", Chandler Tsonda goes for the pin and Mike Wade stays in the ring to protect his pin attempt. One… Two… Three… DING DING DING! Nick: WHAT?! Richard: Chalk up the W. Winner: The A-List The ref raises Tsonda and Wade’s hands as Facey giggles Illustriously from ringside. Jack Murphy comes exploding out of his corner, but all three members of the List spill from opposite sides of the ring. Richard: Brilliant. They were going to stay with quick tags, but when they saw an opportunity, they just acted like the other tag hadn’t happened. Nick: It’s counterintuit…WHAT THE HELL?! Richard: Sure it’s going out of your way to cheat, but they also just took CIV’s first match, did they not? Nick: They covered their own asses in case the ref DID turn around…and then when he didn’t, they just said rules be damned! Richard: It’s compelling TV, I agree. All three members of the List linger outside the ring as Murphy steams and Gildenstern checks on his fallen partner. Wade, Tsonda, and Facey are all grinning hugely and finally turn to leave as "Da Funk" hits, welcoming a great chorus of boos. Nick: *sigh* I just don’t get that team. Richard: Don’t you worry your pretty little head, Nick. ---- Breasts Don't Win Over Everyone The door from the parking lot bursts open and in steps the leather jacket clad Ego of New England. He has something of a snarl plastered on his face along with a dark pair of sunglasses. His forehead is wrinkled in thought and his steps are determined and swift. He turns a couple corners, wanting nothing more than to get to his locker room again to ready himself for the match. He brought his equipment earlier in the day to avoid all the rush and hubbub and now came back to the arena with the show started to ensure everyone is watching the in-ring action. It was his time now. His time to think, to strategize and to plan his victory. Angelica: NITZ! Letting out a sigh, he stopped. He turned with more of a snarl towards the PRIME’s other female interviewer, you know, the one who’s panties he doesn’t have as a souvenir. She jogs up to him, her heels clicking on the floor. Angelica: I just wanted to ask you a question or two. Nitz Donnelly: You know, Faith is gonna shit a brick when she finds out you’re invading her territory. Angelica: Ah whatever. I don’t see her name on you. Nitz Donnelly: Damn straight you don’t, much like I don’t have a sign on my back that reads, "Ask me any fuckin’ question you like before my first major Pay-Per-View match." So since you’ll latch on to my leg and stain my pants if I don’t answer your questions, let’s get this over with. Angelica frowned. She was perhaps beginning to regret tracking down the High Class Ass. She was more used to the guys treating her well because she looked or smelled good. She wasn’t used to being berated in such a way like Donnelly is capable. Angelica: Well, to start, why are you showing up now? Why wouldn’t you be here before now? Nitz Donnelly: Some question... look if you did your homework like a good little girl you would have known that I was here earlier in the day when there was less chance to run into any bimbos, schleps and retards looking for a word, phrase or insult from me. You all think that because I’m fun loving and I have the worst fuckin’ case of Tourette’s syndrome this side of the planet that I’m gonna be willing to talk shit and that I’ll have plenty to say about my opponent. All have tasted the sweetness of victory, the bitterness of defeat and many have found success through their sacrifices. Few, however, have found themselves inducted into the Hall of Fame, an honored roll whose select company has defined PRIME from its past to its present and will be the benchmark for many years in the future. This year, we are honored to induct three long-standing members of the PRIME roster whose contributions, both this year and in the past, have helped set the standard of excellence that PRIME is known for. Last night, in the Cascade Suite of Seattle's Crowne Plaza Hotel, these three men were honored in front of their peers and their fans. I'm pleased to announce the PRIME Hall of Fame Class of 2007. Those months away from PRIME had allowed her to re-evaluate things and realise her flaws. Lavelle: What do you want, Manale? Broken from her train of thought, Charity noted the second name basis and passed it aside. Manale: I just want to say something to you. Lavelle: I’ve a got a match to prepare for. Manale: Look, I drove all the way out here. All I’m asking is for a few minutes of your time. Lavelle smirked and turned his back to her as he placed the black wrist tape on his wrists. Turning toward her, with tape in his mouth as he bit a piece off he noted she had moved just an inch closer. Lavelle: Well I’m sorry you wasted your time. You know how to leave, I assume. Manale was furious, that memorable flare erupting within her. She closed the door and inched closer, much to Lavelle’s disliking. Standing within a few inches of Pierce Lavelle she prodded his chest with her polished finger nail. Manale: Alright, I tried this the nice way, but now you’re going to listen. I know what I did a few months ago was wrong. I didn’t come here to make nice or to reverse my previous mistakes. I remember when you told me that people deserve second chances, no matter what they did, if they accept it and move on. Lavelle began to smile, her finger still a few millimetres from his chest he stepped forward. Manale saw the smile and furrowed her brows, her fuming speech and high pitched voice slowly dying down as she began to calm. Lavelle: You want a water or something? Even more confused now, Manale nodded her head. Manale: That’d be nice … thanks. Lavelle: It might have to wait, Tyler has booked me into the cheapest wing of this stadium. No refreshment and no electricity. Manale: Lisa Tyler is running this place?! Dumbfounded by this new revelation Manale took a step back. She noted that he had begun to relax and saw a smile line his lips. Lavelle: She’s gone mad with power, but she’s just. Manale nodded her head and looked around the room, she noticed the title and smiled. Things had taken a turn from bad to good for Pierce and she felt relieved, considering how things were when she left. The moment Winters had finished their plan. The string of confrontations with Devin Shakur and the increasing tensions with Dusk. And somehow, she had managed to help in the spiteful game against him with Winters. Sitting down, Lavelle looked up toward her. Lavelle: So, Charity, what is it you came here for? Clearing her throat she had gone over this speech a million times in her mind, but as she stood here, those exact words. The plans she had made. Dusk has issues and you were never really one of them, Charity. He hated you because you teamed with Winters. Nick: Is he bored, is he so disconnected from reality that he feels nothing?? He repeats the attack a second time, and then a third, and then as if finally satisfied with his work - or quite possibly, finally bored with it - he hurls the chair at one of the officials standing too close to the ring, and then, finally, makes his exit. The audience showers him in garbage as he makes the journey toward the back, smiling sadistically at officials and guards as he passes them. He makes threatening gestures toward the paramedics when he gets there. Finally, he disappears into the curtain, psychologically slow, letting the entire world know that he’s leaving completely, and utterly, on his own terms. Back in the ring, several people are working on Snow, holding him up by the legs to take the pressure off his throat. Snow seems altogether unresponsive. Finally, the work the chain off his neck and load him onto a stretcher. The audience has gone dead silent one more time as they watch the paramedics hurrying a lifeless Jason Snow off to a waiting ambulance. ---- The End Jonathan Winters paces his locker room, already kitted out in his wrestling tights and rotating his arm to warm himself up for the match ahead. Now is the time to shine and he knows it. Now is the time to raise his game. Now is the time to claim the prize that has thus far eluded him. It started out with Dusk and arguments about a damned finishing manoeuvre. Then Lavelle pushed his way onto the damned scene, seemingly falling in love with Winters’ former manager like the big clown shoes that he is. Finally…Shakur, why he’d gotten his stupid ass involved, Winters even now could not fathom, but he just plain rubbed people up the wrong way. That was three months ago and they’ve been at each others throats ever since. Now is the time to end it. Now is the time to put a stop to this madness and attempt to move on -- the question is, can he? Can any of them? But that doesn’t matter. All that matters is the here and the now. All that matters now is the win. All that matters now is maintaining whatever pride he has left after battling through this hellish ordeal. All that matters now is survival. Matt’s steps carry him to a sole location. The locker room of PRIME’s best. He raps his knuckles on her door and swings it open after he hears the obligatory "It's Open!" Tchu steps inside with a smile to greet the Queen of the Ring, who is sitting on a loveseat with her feet propped up on her bag. She immediately rises to her feet and gives him a smile of her own. Troy: Well, howdy stranger. Tchu: Hey, champ. Troy: Haven't seen much of you lately. Come on in, take a load off. I think you've earned it after that match with Titan. The Inhuman Being adjusts the strap of his gym bag, repositioning it on his shoulder. Tchu: Actually, I'm just headin' out, but I wanted to stop and wish you good luck tonight. It's a helluva feeling having your hand raised at the end of the main event of Colossus. Troy: It's a feeling that I definitely hope to experience not long from now. She purses her lips and eyes him, her face conveying a confused expression. Troy: You're not staying for the rest of the show? That's a bit surprising. You and I are usually here until they turn the lights out. The former two-time Universal Champion can't help but grin. And her words are true... on any other night, he'd be among the last to leave. On more than one occasion The Queen of the Ring and The Inhuman Being had been kicked out of arenas well after the show had ended. Not tonight. Tchu: There's no need to rush on from one show to another. But when there's not a next... it's different. If there was ever a time for a Candid Camera moment, this would be it. Lindsay no longer looks confused. Now, she looks shocked. Positively, jaw-dropped, shocked, so much so that it takes her a moment to say anything. Troy: You're done? Tchu: Sat down with Blair yesterday afternoon and squared away all the paper work. Other than him... no one else knows but you. And I kinda like it that way. Never been the flashy type, ya know? Troy crosses the room and stops in front of him. Troy: Matt, you're really not the quiet type. And after everything you've done, you're just going to silently slip out the back door and wait for Giles to post something on the website tomorrow? That's. I mean. She looks down at the floor, pinches the bridge of her nose and sighs. Troy: Y'know, I came back here for you and Killean. Now he's gone and you've got one foot out the door. I know we all leave at some point but you're not making this easy. Tchu: I'm gonna be around, and I can promise you that I'll stay in touch. Frequently. But, I'm done with the week in and week out grind. I've got a beautiful two-year-old girl who doesn't see near enough of Dad, and I've got another child on the way. Matt rubs the back of his neck, part of it pain from the night's earlier, brutal battle, part of it something else. Something very much not professional wrestling. Tchu: Besides... came up short at UltraViolence, lost a tough one tonight... my best days are behind me. I'm confident I won't be missed too much. This company's in good hands. Matt nods to the Uni title belt, displayed brilliantly amongst Troy's belongings. She follows his gaze to the belt, then offers a soft smile. Troy: I intend to keep it in good hands. She looks back at him. Troy: I hate goodbyes, you know. Tchu: Me too. Which is why i'm trying to keep this as low-key as possible. But, there was one person I figured I owed it to. So, here I am. Troy: Here you are. (She pauses.) You'll have to meet my niece someday. I think she and Georgie would get along good, you know? Maybe even invite the Canuck and Dizzy. Tchu: Sounds like a plan. Kids will probably love it, and I'm sure you and I can keep from goin’ crazy, even with Killer around. Matt turns and looks out into the empty hall, listening to the rumbling of a few voices shouting out orders, trying to keep the biggest show of the year running smooth. Tchu: Well... guess I should be going. Troy: A part of you is going to miss this. Don't think you won't. Tchu: A large part. But Ican live with that. Finally. Matt once again adjusts the bag on his shoulder, pushing it out of the way. Tchu: I'll see ya around, champ. Troy: Make sure that Hall of Fame trophy makes its way through security. Tchu: Will do. With a soft smile, Matt leans forward and the two former AFUC/Team PRIME/Whatever members share a hug. Lindsay pats him on the back a couple times then pulls away. Troy: Take care now. Tchu: You too, Lindz. With a final quick wave, the man who has spent the last two years of his life known to PRIMEates round the world as The Inhuman Being turns and heads down the corridor. At the end of the hall, Matt pushes open the set of double doors that rest calmly under a glowing green sign. ‘EXIT’ He steps out into the light drizzle of a Seattle night, and no one says a thing. No one stops him to ask him where he’s going. Quite the contrast to his explosive arrival in PRIME. Just the way he wants it. Slipping peacefully out the back door. ---- The Ferg Stands Alone Fade in on the familiar PRIME promo backdrop, the old-school setup that usually isn't broken out until the Pay-Per-Views come around. Except there's no Faith, no Angelica and no Matt. There's only Danny, as in Danny Ferguson, as if you couldn't pick him out of a crowd by now. He's dressed in a deep green button-down shirt, untucked, and some stonewashed jeans - the kind of stuff you'd wear if you were doing an EW covershoot, assuming your name was even in EW's Roldex these days. He rests a hand on his hip and rubs the other on his chin, preoccupied in thought, as if he doesn't notice the camera was there. At the bottom corner of the screen, the words "Earlier Today" answer most of our questions. Biting his bottom lip, the Superstar speaks, his voice gravelly and almost hoarse. You'd mistake it to mean that he hadn't slept much lately, but the absence of bags under his eyes betray that. No, this is merely the tone of a man who's said these words countless times in his head, but is now unleashing them upon the world for the first time. Danny Ferguson: I learned a lot from Terry Funk. His eyes never meet the camera, and he remains detached - not "hosting SNL" detached, but "I'm just gonna say this and be done with it" detached. Danny Ferguson: You spend a lot of time with a guy when you're learning how to mimic his mere existence. You pick up a few things. I'm going to keep a few of them secret for now, because you'll probably see them come out in the ring tonight. But one thing I learned was how to play poker. He laughs to himself. His eyes dart around, but still never raise to find the lens. Danny Ferguson: Funker plays five-card draw only. (spot-on Funk impersonation) 'None of that river-turn-television bullshit', he says. He also doesn't bluff. He bets on the cards he was dealt and nothing more. He never fakes it, as he puts it. As you could guess, he doesn't win much with that strategy, especially when everyone else at the table bought their poker face online from Phil Helmuth or some other jackass. But he always walks away from the table feeling content with himself. He could lose his shirt and he'd still stand up and walk away with a genuine smile, because he plays his hand and doesn't fake it. The hand on his chin runs through his hair, ruining and semblance of pre-preened coif. Jittering like a child with ADD, he shifts back and forth between his feet while he mulls over his next words. Danny Ferguson: I will admit it; I had my bluff called a couple months ago. Nova saw my game and he declared shenanigans. I'm not ashamed to admit it. I was once, but I'm not anymore. Once I settled down and remembered what I learned from Terry, I knew I'd been playing it all wrong. So just like he cites me as giving Fuck You new life by attacking him at Rev 122, I ought to give him credit for giving me the enthusiasm and drive to finish what I set out to do months ago. To be painfully poetic, Nova became the catalyst to his own destruction. He turns slightly, like he was finished and walking away, but he turns back, clearly holding something back. After the kind of hand-wringing and half-words that are normally reserved for breaking up with someone. Finally, he nods to himself, sure that everything's in the right order. Danny Ferguson: I think I upset a lot of people by dropping the act. I can't blame them, but I'm also not going to drive myself crazy trying to win them over to my side. To channel my inner Stuart Smalley, I believe in me. The Funker's table rhetoric has a lesson beyond fleecing your buddies every Wednesday night: Terry, more than anyone, knows that you've gotta do what feels right to you. Stick to what you believe and let the chips - or the fans - fall where they may. So I sincerely apologize to those of you who somehow feel shorted by my behavior of the last several weeks. However, do not think for a moment that I betrayed you. I didn't betray the fans, or Lindsay, or Nova, or anyone. I just stayed true to myself. Finally, his chin raises and his eyes lock with the lens. The focus alone is enough to shatter the glass. Shaking just slightly, he swallows hard and presses forward. Danny Ferguson: I think everyone watching knows the feeling. You get cut off in traffic, or shorted on a tip, or get beer spilled on you by some drunk at a baseball game. Yeah, those are some awfully petty examples, but it never feels petty to the person holding the grudge. When you're on the short end, and you want to right the wrongs, it doesn't quite matter how trivial it seems to anyone else. You want to to go one up on that person who did wrong by you. Unblinking and instantly ferocious, his eyes burrow through the camera and into your chest. The shaking throws the autofocus of the lens in and out of proper alignment, but it comes back to the correct position just before he speaks again. Danny Ferguson: How far will I go, and at what point will I be satisfied? I can't pretend to know anymore. What matters to me know is that even if I lose my shirt out there at the table, I can still keep my chin high when I leave it. My cards are on the table; it's time to let the hand play itself out. With the final words and one deep, blinking breath, Danny disappears from the frame, leaving only the backdrop. The click-slide of his heels on the cement signal his departure. The "Earlier Today" title fades out, and then the scene does the same, plunging back into the darkness. ---- Nova vs. Danny Ferguson The camera pans around the stadium as the fans are already cheering because they know what time it is. And it ain’t Tool Time. It’s Nova/D-Fergs time. And what a sexy time it’s about to be. Nick: I know it’s cliché, Rich, but I just don’t know how else to say it: What. A. Night. The biggest show of the year and it’s lived up to and surpassed our wildest… Richard: Your wildest expectations, Nick. Yours. To me, we still have two matches to go and the outcomes of these matches will directly impact how much of my mood-stabilizing medicine I have to wash down with cheap vodka after the show. Danny and Sonny win…meh, two pills. Nova and Troy…God, I could go as high as sixteen. Nick: As much as I’d hate to see your stomach pumped again, Richard, I have to cross my fingers and hope that the Queen of the Ring and the Risen Star get their justice tonight! And we get to answer one of those questions, fans, right now! The arena darkens as the Wal*Tron comes to life, the screen black as horizontal white lines a la the old movie reels of yestercentury. The opening chords of the Beatles’ "Yesterday" float out of the P.A. system. "Yesterday…" In slow-motion black and white, Nova and Danny stand across the ring from one another at ReVolution 98. Cut to Nova hitting a standing dropkick on Danny as the A-Lister is halfway through a gorgeous moonsault off the top rope. Cut to Danny, delivering a Box Office Bomb on the Risen Star on top of a steel chair. "…all my troubles seemed so far away…" Nova stands on the wrestlers’ side of the ramp headed down towards the ring, leaned over into the fans, whose arms are around him, slapping him on the back and cheering voraciously. A fan behind him holds up a sign that reads "Nova: Face of the Year 2006!" and Nova points to it as he looks into the camera, grinning widely. "…now it looks as though they’re here to stay… Cut to Danny wide-eyed, babbling hysterically as police drag him from the building at ReVolution 107. They shove him into a police car and he turns around to face the cameras, hands pressed against the back windshield as camera flashes illuminate his frightened face. Nova stands in the distance, looking on with interest as the cadre of squad cars pull out of the parking garage. "Oh, I believe…in yesterday…" The mellow guitars are suddenly distorted, slowing down into a low, monotone hum before the chorus of the Deftones’ "Change (In the House of Flies)" erupts over the arena sound system. "I watched a change…in you…" The picture reverts to color as Nova pulls himself up off the mat at the Great American Nightmare 2006, running a hand through his sweaty mop as the realization of defeat sinks in. In the unfocused background, Tchu raises the Universal Title in the air. Cut to Nova standing in the middle of the ring at King of Kings, surrounded by Angelo Deville, Rich Rollins, and Jason Snow. His locks shorn, he raises the Universal Title in the air as confetti rains down from the rafters. Cut to Danny Ferguson in a jail cell, peeking through the bars as a guard stationed nearby watches the Pay-Per-View on a small television. "It’s like you never had wings…" Nova, Snow, Deville, and Tony Gamble putting the boots to Ferguson in the ring at ReVolution 117. Cut to Nova tied to a chair, ravenously attempting to escape as Danny paces around him with a baseball bat. Cut to Danny sliding under the bottom rope in the ring at ReVolution 122 as Nova stands over Chet Worth, and bashing the Risen Star with a chair across the back. "Now you feel so alive…" Cut to Nova standing in the ring with Snow, Gamble, and the former Mr. Silver, Chairman of PRIME, soaking in the ire of the fans. Cut to Danny huddled in a dressing room backstage with Killean Sirrajin, Lindsay Troy, and Tchu. Cut to Danny and Troy on the entrance ramp at ReVolution 129, Troy drenched in sweat holding the Universal Title over her shoulder and Danny sharing a venomous stare with Nova, who remains in the ring. "I watched you change…" In rapid succession, Nova is shown kicking Danny’s chair leg out from under him, then Nova hitting the Bourbon for Breakfast on him at Overkill, then Danny jumping the Risen Star from behind in the ring, then Nova and Danny brawling in the crowd at ReVolution 133, then Nova swinging a chair wildly at the reformed A-List in the ring, then finally close-ups of Nova’s face as he stands in the ring, and Danny flustered on the entrance ramp, spittle flying from his mouth as Chandler Tsonda and the Illustrious Face-Eater hold him back. The screen goes black and then a series of words appear in white block letters. NOVA. DANNY FERGUSON. COLOSSUS IV… …NOW. The lights come up. Nick: A moving montage of the rollercoaster rides that have been the lives and careers of Nova and Danny Ferguson, especially in the year’s time that has elapsed since Colossus III! Richard: Yeah…moving…it’s "moving" people towards the exit signs! Let’s see some action! Nick: Something we can agree on! Nova vs. Danny Ferguson, at long last, and it’s about to go down in that very ring! Tonight's the night that lights go out in Safeco, so shield the faces of your epileptic children and bear with the theatrics, mkay? Save for the glowing LED sponsor ads, the floor of the arena goes black and a roar comes up. The second of the triple main events is upon them. Amidst the darkness, a small army of stagehands and crew workers burst from the back, rolling an aisle-wide red carpet out from entrance to right at the ring. Once it's done and the group clears out, the spinning lights along the entrance all begin to pulse, changing green to yellow to white to green. And then the music kicks in... Spotlights circle the crowd as the beat kicks in. At the very first chorus of "YEEEEAAAH" a blinding set of lights erupt on the entrance stage, casting shadows all across the stadium and unveiling Danny Ferguson standing at the very head of the aisle. The color scheme has changed just slightly for this evening. His robe and boots are primarily white with green and gold highlights, a more angelic change from the norm. His red hair and orange-tan skin stand out sharply against the pure white. With barely a photo-op pause atop the stage, Danny heads for the ring, somewhere between limping and pimping. Behind him, the bodyguards keep a watchful eye to the crowd, the spotlights accentuating the green pinstripes in their deep grey suits. The group moves in tune with the beat, with the spotlight following every step of the way. With each "YEAH" the blinding lights on the stage pulse, throwing the white balance of the cameras off and bathing the Superstar in an aura befitting of his attire. As the crew approaches each set of track lights along the aisle, they convert to flashing strobes, popping like flashbulbs of a camera. One thing is certain for the entirety of this occasionally blinding and perpetually loud arrival upon the scene: All eyes are on Danny Ferguson. The Superstar reaches the ring and jumps up onto the apron. He quickly sheds the robe to reveal a pair of tights with the same pristine white color scheme. With the music dying off he runs the ropes a few times and rolls out his shoulders before turning his attention back to the entrance way. The lights remain down as the Wal*Tron fades into the familiar field of stars, and screens overhead scatter thousands of tiny white lights out across the crowd, replicating the field of stars onscreen in their midst, and across the body and face of a deadly serious Danny Ferguson in the ring. The fans to erupt into cheers as the voice of Master of Ceremonies For Life George Clinton booms over the loudspeakers. "Mother Earth is pregnant for the third time, for y’all have knocked her up. I have tasted the maggots in the mind of the universe, and I was not offended…for I knew I had to rise above it all…or drown in my own shit." The field of stars melt togethert to form the word "NOVA" as "Maggot Brain (Live ’71)" blasts out of the PA system in sync with the blue and white pyrotechnics that explode around the Wal*Tron. As the smoke from them clears, a lone spotlight beams down onto the floor-level stage through the fog, where the Risen Star is knelt, one fist raised into the air. As always, the light catches smoke wafting up irridescently from the burning end of the cigarette jutting out of Nova’s mouth, rising above his head and joining with the heavier smoke of the PPV pyro. The Risen Star rises to his feet and the spotlight follows him as he makes his way to the ring, sparing a small grin every once in a while at fans who cheer him on. Nick: Nova opting out of the grandiose Colossus entrance this year, it would appear – at Colossus III you’ll remember he was carried to the ring, along with two random drunk girls, on a motorized dais – save for the traditional unique pay-per-view tights that Danny’s also sporting! Richard: This isn’t radio, Nick. People can see the damn tights for themselves. Nick: Well, what I was getting at is that perhaps Nova’s basic entrance to the biggest show of the year is meant to highlight what he said to Danny at ReV 135…that he would make the Box Office Smash a wrestler tonight…that tonight would be about the wrestling. Richard: Hmph. Maybe. The spotlight following Nova to the ring illuminates his new silver tights with black-outlined white lightning bolts coursing down both legs. His knee and armpads are also silver. Nova reaches the ring and spares the slide-under, opting for the stairs as his eyes leave the fans around him and focus on the man in the ring. Not Vince Howard. Not Anakin or Kenobi or Yoda. Danny Fer-no, not the referee. Yup, there…Danny Ferguson. The Risen Star steps through the ropes and the spotlight dies away as the ref stands between the PRIME veterans, giving them the formal match stipulation. Nick: Remember, fans, per Nova’s request…a possible three falls. The first decided by pin. The second decided by submission…and the final fall, if it comes to it, by a standing ten count that will be issued until only one man can answer it. Richard: Nailing his own coffin, if you ask me. Nick: That confident in Danny Ferguson, are we? Richard: Absolutely. Somewhere in the crowd a "NO-VA! *clap, clap* NO-VA! *clap-clap*" chant starts up, and the Risen Star offers the fans a smile. The ref calls for the bell and steps back as the crowd cheers for the start of the match. Nick: It’s on, and…WHOA! The sounds of the bell might still be echoing above the roar of the fans as Nova charges across the ring and springs into mid-air, colliding with Ferguson forearm outstretched, driving the star of More Than Hardcore violently backwards into the turnbuckle post. Nick: NOVA GOING FROM ZERO TO ONE HUNDRED HERE, in the opening seconds of the match! Richard: GAH~! Call it off! Restart, restart!! Danny’s surprise is evident as he attempts to hold out his arms and block the barrage of stiff shots coming from the fists, forearms, and elbows of the Risen Star. Two quick forearm smashes to the left side of his head, followed by a European uppercut, and Ferguson’s on Dream St., slumped back against the post pads as Nova steps back and then drives a knee into his stomach. Nick: The last time Nova got Danny to himself, as we saw for a moment in that video earlier, was well over a year ago, and it feels like ever since they’ve been waiting to tear into one another again…and right now Nova isn’t wasting a minute of it! Richard: Start wasting, dammit! Start wasting!! Nova grabs Danny and slings him away from the ropes. Danny runs across the ring towards the other turnbuckle post but manages to put out the brakes, skidding to a stop before swerving around drunkenly…only to catch a neck-height short-arm clothesline from the Risen Star that whips him head-first down to the mat. Nova drops with him, landing an elbow across Ferguson’s sternum that sends the latter rolling out of the ring, clutching his chest and gasping for air. Nova stands up, motioning for Danny to re-enter, and the fans cheer uproariously for the opening assault. Danny looks around at them for a second before turning his wide-eyed "deer in headlights" expression back on his nemesis. Nick: Danny Ferguson doesn’t know what to think right now! Whatever he expected coming into the match, it wasn’t this…although maybe he should have! Richard: It’s alright, D-Fergs…take a breath, think about this… Which is exactly what Danny does, pacing for a moment before sliding back under the ropes. Nova rushes forward again, but Ferguson picks it and turns to the side, sweeping Nova’s leg out with one arm before springing up and dropping a rolling knee across his chest. He doesn’t move back in, opting to roll away and onto his feet as Nova shakes it off and kips back up. Richard: Attaboy! That’s the game of Danny Ferguson, Nick, and it’s what will win this match, mark my words! Nova can forage ahead blindly, but D-Fergs has a map and a compass and he’ll pick his target! Nova moves in again, teasing at a tie-up before shoving Danny against the ropes. As Ferguson bounces off, Nova steps to the side, whipping the former hard across the chest with a knife-edge chop. The fans "WOOOO~!" dutifully, and Danny howls, clutching his chest for a moment before Nova pushes his arms away and connects with a second chop. Nick: Vicious chops from the Risen Star here, and Danny’s chest is starting to resemble… Richard: …his hair! AH-HAR-HAR-HAR-HAR-HAR!!! God, I’ve heard so many "Danny Ferguson has red hair" jokes in the last couple of years, sometimes I lie in bed at night thinking about and vomit on my stomach. Nick: Noooo, that’s called alcoholism. Richard: *Sob* I need help… Danny steps away from the ropes, still trying to protect his chest which by now has, from the looks of it, no blood vessels left intact. Again and again Nova chops him, the two now making a circle around the edge of the ring as the Risen Star pursues and Danny stumbles away in front of him. Thwack! Thwack! Thwack! Whipping his cupped hand around for what has to be the twentieth time, Nova’s eyes widen as Danny catches it, releasing only to spin 360 degrees and land a discus punch between the former Universal Champion’s eyes, sending little heavenly bodies into his field of vision and his own body down to the mat. Richard: HA! What an idiot! I swear, a decade in the industry and Nova still doesn’t know that if you chop a guy twenty times, be ready for him to dodge the twenty-first! Nick: Oh, sorry, didn’t realize I was sitting next to the legendary Richard Parker, trainer of the stars. Richard: I’ve stepped between the ropes a few times in my day. Nick: Attempting to get action from a woman knocked unconscious by a piledriver by diving into the ring at a Mid South house show does not count, Rich. Richard: Why didn’t that ever work in real life the way it did in my head? Danny puts a few boots to Nova’s head before hoisting him up to his feet. He grabs Nova for a scoop slam, but the Risen Star shoves him backwards against the ropes, hooking him as he comes back and tossing him overhead with a cradle suplex. Richard: Argh! Nick: This is where Nova has always shined, Richard, as a "sprinter," if you will, inside the ring. In the opening minutes of a contest he’s as ferocious as any man – or woman – I’ve probably ever seen. Richard: Just remember, there are at least two, maybe three falls in this match, and as much as your golden boy’s shining now, let him exhaust that energy until air isn’t coming as easily into those deflated tires he calls lungs! Then we’ll see! Nick: You’re right to point that out. It’s always been one of Nova’s biggest criticisms. That and the whole ‘endorsement of drugs’ thing. Nova dives on Ferguson and pulls him up, hooking both arms for a double underhook DDT that never connects head to mat as Danny pushes Nova back, driving the small of the Risen Star’s back against the turnbuckle post forcefully. When Nova won’t release his DDT headlock Danny rears back and rams his shoulder into Nova’s stomach once…twice…and on the third, Nova releases, sagging slightly in the corner. Danny steps back, blood rushing from his head to the rest of his body, and then begins peppering the Risen Star with rights and occasional lefts. Dazed, Nova can do little to halt the barrage, and seeing this, Danny climbs up onto the first rope and looks out into the fans, shouting something inaudible that gets a mixed response before raising his fist. Nick: I don’t know if it’s so wise to take the one moment Danny’s been in control practically the entire match so far and use it showing off to the fans… Richard: Wrestling is half mental, Nick! Danny’s sending a message! Nick: Half mental? Richard: Forty percent? Nick: … Richard: Thirty-eight, and that’s as low as I go. Nick: Oh, shut up, already. Danny takes another look at his raised fist before bringing it down on Nova’s head. The fans count in time with each blow… Fans: ONE! TWO! THREE! FOUR! FIVE! SIX! SEVEN! EIG-OHHHHH!! …but don’t reach a full ten as Nova pushes Danny’s legs out with his arms, sending Ferguson falling down face-first onto the turnbuckle post. Nova shakes away the blows and rolls around Danny’s side, arms reaching for his waist and the follow-up German suplex…but Danny blindly mule-kicks backwards, luckily (for him) burying his heel in Nova’s crotch. The Risen Star stumbles back a few steps before falling to the mat, hands clutching the jewels that, thanks to a few years in this ‘Nad-Forsaken place, will probably never function correctly again. Nick: Oh, COME ON! That’s worth a warning, or…or something, isn’t it? Richard: Okay, first…the ref was behind them. No way to tell what happened. Second, you’re gonna, what, end a co-Main Event on the biggest night of PRIME’s calendar year…because of a kick in the balls? Nick: (Sighing) No, I guess not. But I find it difficult to believe Ferguson intended anything else but exactly what happened there. Richard: Ummm…that’s because he didn’t intend anything else. Nova rolls over on the mat, doing the bicycle kicks with his legs that somehow always make it a little better, and Danny wastes no time stepping forward, swinging a leg out, and dropping an elbow down onto the former Universal Champion’s shoulder. He grabs Nova by the hair (yes, it’s grown back, regardless of what that poser looks like) and yanks him up, scooping him into the air and running across the ring before slinging him around and planting him on the canvas with a powerslam. Nick: Wow! Impressive offense from Danny Ferguson, who’s trying to shift the momentum in this match that has, so far, been all Nova! Richard: People forget how strong the guy is, Nick…Danny can hoist guys twice his size overhead. Nick: Ferguson going for something big here, and this from a guy who does not generally include much of the aerial in his offense… Richard: It’s COLOSSUS, baby…ya gotta risk big to win big. Danny raises his arms, taking a deep breath as he’s bathed in the light of flashbulbs…and a few thousand people will treasure forever their photos of him landing crotch-first on the top turnbuckle as Nova springs off the mat and shoves one of his legs out. Danny’s mouth hangs open, the oxygen emptying out of his lungs, and Nova gets a breath of his own, remaining on his knees. Ferguson wobbles for a second and then tumbles head-first into the ring…and onto Nova’s waiting shoulders who hoists him back up, spins around, and runs across the ring before spiking the star of Hard Money onto the mat with a sit-out powerbomb. Nick: And Nova answers Danny’s display of power with one of his own! Innovative counter-offense from the Risen Star! Richard: Using fragments! Nova tosses Danny’s legs off of him and gets up, walking to the corner and climbing to the top rope before perching himself there as the fans around that area of the ring stand up and begin cheering. Nick: Nova also mirroring Danny’s attempt at a top-rope maneuver…and he’s airborne, curving back…and LANDING that beautiful shooting star press on Danny Ferguson! And you have to appreciate the symbolism, Richard, because I don’t think we’ve seen a shooting star press even attempted from Nova since that career-changing injury two years ago at Tropical Turmoil at the hands of Brandon Youngblood! Richard: I don’t have to appreciate a damn thing. Nova stays on for the cover… Ref: ONE! . . . . . . TWO! . . . . . . TH- Danny shoots a shoulder up, and Nova pulls himself up to his knees before grabbing a handful of Ferguson’s hair and lighting up the side of his face with a string of right hands. Once the birdies are obviously circling Danny’s head, Nova pulls him to his feet and slings him into the corner. Danny hits the top turnbuckle chest-first and stumbles backwards into Nova’s arms, who lifts him for a backdrop that doesn’t hit as Danny flips backwards over Nova’s head and lands on his feet. In the relative quiet of the parking garage – a sanctuary, if you will, from the chaos enveloping the rest of Safeco Field – two security guards maintain their vigilant watch over the vehicles nicer than their own belonging to people more rich and famous than they could ever hope to get drunk and sleep with. Tall Guard: How lame. I hear people screaming in the distance and stuff, and we’re here guarding a bunch of a cars nicer than ours belonging to people more rich and famous than we could ever hope to get drunk and sleep with. Fat Guard: Actually, I’ve worked a lot of shows like this, and crazy shit goes down in the parking garage. Tall Guard: Really? Fat Guard: Sometimes. Once, I worked an Ozzy show, and the tour bus pulled up in the garage, and out steps Osbourne followed by The Man Himself…JESUS CHRIST! Tall Guard: No shit?! Jesus was partying with Ozzy?! When he doesn’t get an answer from his comically disproportionate partner, Tall Guard looks at him. Fat Guard’s eyes are wide and his jaw hangs open; he’s frozen in his tracks. Tall Guard turns slowly, following the line of sight around until a blast of hot air is blown into his face, knocking his cap off. Trembling, he opens his eyes slowly, beholding with equal parts terror and wonder the enormous snout of a huge white stallion, its nose (and the source of the sudden exhalation) only inches from his face. The jingling of beads hanging from the horse’s saddle can be heard, and a dreamcatcher swings beneath the halter around its snout. Tall Guard follows the horse’s elongated face up, up, up past its knowing eyes, large ears and tuft of hair poking away from its crown, to the rider looming several feet above both guards, machete strapped to his back, eyes boring down into them from behind a face marked by battle scars and wrinkles of time… …"The Wolf" Mike Randalls. Both guards find their tongues as useless in this moment as the unloaded weapons at their hips. Randalls senses the communication breakdown. Mike Randalls: I’m waiting for someone. This seems to jar them. Tall Guard: You, uh, you…you… Randalls cocks an eyebrow at him. Tall Guard: …you…you, um, can’t have a horse in the parking garage! Mike Randalls: I don’t. Tall Guard: You…don’t? Mike Randalls: No. I have two horses in the parking garage. The Wolf whistles loudly, and a second broad-shouldered steed trots out of the shadows, its coat mostly a rich brown, but with white in its snout and on the tufts of hair around its hooves. Tall Guard: Ohhhh, my God. T-t-two horses in the…parking…um, sir, I have to ask you to… He trails off, whatever authority he thought he had dissipating as his gaze meets that of the Wolf once again. Randalls could tell him that he’d fought alongside Sonoran desert warriors in conflicts born of nothing more than the desire to inflict and receive pain, that he’d once driven a stake through a man’s leg in the middle of a wrestling ring, that he’d seen universes bloat and implode on themselves, oblivious to his observance, or that while on a potent mixture of peyote buttons and Colombia’s finest he’d witnessed a German dwarf stuff himself up to the ankle inside a Samoan prostitute’s unholiest of unholies… …but his cold steely gaze says all that and more. Fat Guard: (putting a hand on his comrade’s shoulder) Why don’t we just go somewhere for a while? Before Tall Guard can acquiesce, the double doors leading inside the arena are shoved open and Nova walks down the concrete steps, a duffel bag hanging from one hand. Nova: Don’t worry, guys, he’s with me. Mike Randalls: No, he’s with me. However it works out, the guards get the picture. Nodding to the Risen Star, they make themselves scarce. Nova lights a cigarette. Nova: These horses are friggin’ sweet. (Looking back at the building) Man...I have a lot of fuckin’ history with this place. As if summoned by that last comment, the doors are pushed open a second time, and the Queen of the Ring joins the party, dressed to go to battle with Sonny Silver. She stops abruptly at the sight of the Wolf holding the reins now of both horses. Lindsay Troy: Mike? Randalls regards her with a tip of his drifter's hat, but not much else. Lindsay Troy: I'd ask what you're doing here, but ... yeah, you've got the machete. I know better. The Wolf's emerald gaze doesn't waver and Troy shakes off the look, turning her attention to Nova. She walks down the steps towards him. Lindsay Troy: I'm getting ready to go pound Sonny's face in and a runner tells me that the match against Danny was your last one. Did I hear that right? You're done too? I managed to handle one retirement announcement already from Matt but at least he had the decency to stop by before walking away. The Risen Star stares down at the ground for a moment, taking a long drag from his cigarette, before bringing his weary blue eyes back up to meet Troy’s gaze. Nova: You're right, I'm sorry, I should have stopped by or told you. It's just…it's been a rough year, Lindz. Every thing about this place summons painful memories and reminds me of…me, I guess, and all the shitty things I've done. I have to get away from it, or I'm pretty sure this place'll be, quite literally, the death of me. I'm headed south for a while, first to Mexico and then who knows? Central or South America, maybe… Lindsay looks at Randalls again, then back to Nova, then at the ground. Lindsay Troy: (nodding slowly) It’s probably for the best. Nova cocks an eyebrow as he pitches his cigarette away. Nova: Really? Of all the reactions I’d contemplated, that was not one of them. Lindsay Troy: (looking up at him) Please, Caesar. Nova stares at her for a moment then grabs her hand, pulling her in and planting a quick kiss on her mouth. He steps back as Troy’s face reddens in the dim lighting. Mike Randalls: The Astral Plane is waiting, Eagle Star. Nova glances back at Randalls, giving him the "dude, just a second" look before turning his gaze back to Troy. Lindsay Troy: I’ll, um...I'll....see you in the New Frontier, then, I guess. Nova: I think I’ll probably see you first. Thanks for everything, Lindz… He hoists his duffel bag up onto the horse’s back, behind the saddle, and then puts a foot in the stirrup. He spares Lindsay another glance... Nova: …everything. …and pulls himself up onto the horse, looking over to the Wolf. Nova: Shall we? Randalls nods, and puts the boots to the side of his steed. Mike Randalls: HYAAH!
